The design enables critical indices to be preferentially placed on faster (premium-tier) nodes while maintaining graceful degradation when premium capacity is exhausted.\n\n## Deliverables\n\n### 1. **design_doc.md** - Comprehensive Design Document\n\nA complete design specification covering:\n\n- **Current Architecture Overview**: Detailed analysis of existing allocation framework\n  - ShardsAllocator interface and BalancedShardsAllocator implementation\n  - AllocationDecider base class and composition pattern\n  - Existing decider examples (DiskThresholdDecider, AwarenessAllocationDecider)\n\n- **Problem Statement**: Why priority-aware allocation is needed\n  - Current lack of performance-tier awareness\n  - Limitation in handling critical vs. non-critical indices\n\n- **Proposed Design**: Four-component solution\n  - `index.routing.allocation.priority` setting (0-100, default 50)\n  - `node.attr.allocation_tier` attribute (e.g., \"premium\", \"standard\")\n  - `PriorityAllocationDecider` - enforces priority constraints\n  - `PriorityShardsAllocator` - reorders shards by priority for allocation\n\n- **Integration Points**: How new components compose with existing allocation framework\n  - Placement in the decider chain\n  - Interaction with DiskThresholdDecider, AwarenessAllocationDecider, FilterAllocationDecider\n  - Backward compatibility and graceful degradation\n\n- **API Specification**: New settings and configuration\n  - Index settings: `index.routing.allocation.priority`\n  - Cluster settings: `cluster.routing.allocation.priority.*`\n  - Cluster Explain API enhancements\n\n- **Implementation Roadmap**: 4-phase development plan\n- **Behavior Examples**: Real-world scenarios with expected outcomes\n- **Testing Strategy**: Unit, integration, and chaos test plans\n- **References**: Links to relevant Elasticsearch source files\n\n**Key Design Principles**:\n1. Non-intrusive - integrates without major refactoring\n2. Composable - works alongside existing deciders\n3. Configurable - allows operator control and tuning\n4. Observable - clear logging and explain API output\n5. Safe - graceful degradation without data loss\n\n### 2. **PriorityShardsAllocator.java** - Skeleton Implementation\n\nA Java class implementing the `ShardsAllocator` interface that:\n\n- **Wraps BalancedShardsAllocator**: Delegates actual balancing decisions\n- **Sorts Shards by Priority**: Reorders unassigned shards queue (highest priority first)\n- **Supports Async Allocation**: Implements both sync and async allocation methods\n- **Dynamic Configuration**: Watches for runtime setting changes\n- **Graceful Error Handling**: Continues with standard allocation on errors\n\n**Key Methods**:\n- `allocate(RoutingAllocation)` - sorts shards then delegates\n- `allocate(RoutingAllocation, ActionListener<Void>)` - async variant\n- `sortUnassignedShardsByPriority()` - reorders shard queue\n- `getIndexPriority()` - extracts priority from index metadata\n- `isNodePremiumTier()` - classifies node as premium/standard\n\n**Settings Exposed**:\n- `cluster.routing.allocation.priority.enabled` (Boolean, default: true)\n- `cluster.routing.allocation.priority.premium_tiers` (List[String], default: [\"premium\"])\n- `cluster.routing.allocation.priority.threshold` (Integer 0-100, default: 70)\n\n### 3. **PriorityAllocationDecider.java** - Skeleton Implementation\n\nA Java class extending `AllocationDecider` that:\n\n- **Enforces Priority Constraints**: Returns YES/THROTTLE/NO based on priority and tier\n- **Decision Logic**:\n  - High-priority shard on premium node → YES\n  - High-priority shard on standard node → THROTTLE (soft constraint)\n  - Low-priority shard on any node → YES\n- **Composes with Other Deciders**: Fails fast on NO from other deciders\n- **Dynamic Configuration**: Watches for runtime setting changes\n- **Detailed Logging**: Trace-level logging for debugging allocation decisions\n\n**Key Methods**:\n- `canAllocate(ShardRouting, RoutingNode, RoutingAllocation)` - main decision logic\n- `canRemain()` - uses same logic as canAllocate\n- `getIndexPriority()` - extracts priority from index metadata\n- `isNodePremiumTier()` - classifies node tier\n\n**Settings Exposed**:\n- Same as PriorityShardsAllocator\n\n## Architecture Diagram\n\n```\nAllocationService (orchestration)\n    ↓\nShardsAllocator (pluggable)\n    ├── BalancedShardsAllocator (existing)\n    └── PriorityShardsAllocator (NEW)\n            ↓\n        [reorder shards by priority]\n            ↓\n        BalancedShardsAllocator.allocate()\n            ↓\n        AllocationDeciders chain\n            ├── EnableAllocationDecider\n            ├── DiskThresholdDecider\n            ├── AwarenessAllocationDecider\n            ├── FilterAllocationDecider\n            ├── PriorityAllocationDecider (NEW)\n            └── ... other deciders ...\n```\n\n## Design Highlights\n\n### 1. Non-Intrusive Integration\n\nThe design extends existing components rather than modifying them:\n- PriorityShardsAllocator wraps BalancedShardsAllocator (decorator pattern)\n- PriorityAllocationDecider extends AllocationDecider (template method pattern)\n- New settings follow Elasticsearch conventions\n\n### 2. Composability with Existing Deciders\n\nPriority constraints interact safely with other constraints:\n- **DiskThreshold** (hard constraint): Disk space checks take precedence\n- **Awareness** (hard constraint): Rack/zone distribution respected within priority preferences\n- **Filter** (hard constraint): Include/exclude/require filters applied first\n- **Priority** (soft constraint): THROTTLE signals preference, not requirement\n\n### 3. Graceful Degradation\n\nHigh-priority shards still allocate when premium nodes unavailable:\n- THROTTLE decision allows allocation to standard nodes\n- Falls back to standard balanced allocation\n- No starvation; all shards eventually allocate\n\n### 4. Dynamic Configuration\n\nAll settings are dynamic (cluster-scoped):\n- No cluster restart required to enable/disable\n- Can adjust premium tiers and thresholds at runtime\n- Easy A/B testing of configurations\n\n## Configuration Examples\n\n### Enable Priority Allocation\n\n```bash\nPUT /_cluster/settings\n{\n  \"persistent\": {\n    \"cluster.routing.allocation.priority.enabled\": true,\n    \"cluster.routing.allocation.priority.premium_tiers\": [\"premium\", \"hot\"],\n    \"cluster.routing.allocation.priority.threshold\": 70\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### Configure Node Tiers\n\nIn `elasticsearch.yml`:\n```yaml\n# Premium nodes (fast SSD, 256GB RAM)\nnode.name: premium-node-1\nnode.attr.allocation_tier: premium\n\n# Standard nodes (regular SSD, 64GB RAM)\nnode.name: standard-node-1\nnode.attr.allocation_tier: standard\n```\n\n### Set Index Priority\n\n```bash\nPUT /critical-app/_settings\n{\n  \"index.routing.allocation.priority\": 100\n}\n\nPUT /cache-index/_settings\n{\n  \"index.routing.allocation.priority\": 10\n}\n```\n\n## Usage Scenarios\n\n### Scenario 1: Critical Application Index\n\n```\nIndex: critical-app\n- priority: 100 (highest)\n- 3 shards, 2 replicas each\n\nCluster:\n- 2 premium nodes (fast SSD, 256GB RAM)\n- 3 standard nodes (regular SSD, 64GB RAM)\n\nResult:\n- Primary shards prefer premium nodes\n- Replica shards distributed across both tiers\n- If premium nodes full, gracefully degrade to standard\n```\n\n### Scenario 2: Multi-Tier Allocation\n\n```\nIndices:\n- prod-primary (priority: 100)  → premium nodes\n- prod-replica (priority: 80)   → premium or standard\n- staging (priority: 40)        → any node\n- logs (priority: 10)           → standard nodes\n\nAllocation Strategy:\n1. ✅ `allocate(RoutingAllocation)` - sync allocation with priority sorting\n   - ✅ `allocate(RoutingAllocation, ActionListener<Void>)` - async variant\n   - ✅ `execute(RoutingAllocation, AllocationCommands, ...)` - command execution\n   - ✅ `decideShardAllocation(ShardRouting, RoutingAllocation)` - explain API support\n\n2. **Configuration Settings**\n   - `CLUSTER_ROUTING_ALLOCATION_PRIORITY_ENABLED` - feature toggle\n   - reorders shard queue by priority\n   - `getIndexPriority()` - extracts priority from IndexMetadata\n   - `isNodePremiumTier()` - classifies node as premium/standard\n   - Configuration update listeners for dynamic changes\n\n4. **Documentation**\n   - ✅ `canAllocate(ShardRouting, RoutingNode, RoutingAllocation)` - main decision logic\n   - ✅ `canRemain(IndexMetadata, ShardRouting, RoutingNode, RoutingAllocation)` - residency decision\n   - Uses return Decision.YES, Decision.THROTTLE, or Decision.NO\n\n2. **Decision Logic**\n   ```\n   if feature disabled:\n       return YES (always allow)\n\n   if priority < threshold:\n       return YES (no constraint)\n\n   if priority >= threshold:\n       if node is premium:\n           return YES (preferred allocation)\n       else:\n           return THROTTLE (allow but prefer premium)\n   ```\n\n3. **Configuration Settings**\n   - `CLUSTER_ROUTING_ALLOCATION_PRIORITY_ENABLED` - feature toggle\n   - `CLUSTER_ROUTING_ALLOCATION_PRIORITY_PREMIUM_TIERS` - tier list\n   - `CLUSTER_ROUTING_ALLOCATION_PRIORITY_THRESHOLD` - soft constraint threshold\n\n4. **Core Methods**\n   - `getIndexPriority()` - extracts priority from IndexMetadata\n   - `isNodePremiumTier()` - checks node tier attribute\n   - Configuration update listeners for dynamic changes\n\n5. **Documentation**\n   - Comprehensive JavaDoc with decision logic explanation\n   - Detailed class documentation with usage examples\n   - Example scenarios with expected outcomes\n   - Integration notes with other deciders\n\n6. **Error Handling & Logging**\n   - Graceful degradation with sensible defaults\n   - Trace-level logging for each decision\n   - Debug logging for configuration updates\n\n---\n\n## Deliverable #4: Overview & Integration Guide\n**File**: `README.md` (314 lines)\n\n### Contents:\n1. **Project Overview** - Two-Component Architecture\n- **PriorityShardsAllocator**: Reorders shards (macro-level)\n- **PriorityAllocationDecider**: Makes node-level decisions (micro-level)\n- **Benefit**: Clean separation of concerns, composable with existing components\n\n### 2. Soft Constraint via THROTTLE\n- High-priority shards on standard nodes return THROTTLE (not NO)\n- **Benefit**: Graceful degradation when premium capacity exhausted\n- **Safety**: No starvation of critical indices\n\n### 3. Dynamic Configuration\n- All settings cluster-scoped and dynamic\n- **Benefit**: No restart required; supports A/B testing\n- **Simplicity**: Follows Elasticsearch patterns\n\n### 4. Non-Intrusive Integration\n- Wraps BalancedShardsAllocator (decorator pattern)\n- Extends AllocationDecider (template method pattern)\n- **Benefit**: No modification of existing code\n- **Safety**: Minimal risk of regression\n\n### 5. Node Tier via Attributes\n- Uses existing `node.attr.*` mechanism\n- Operators define tier names (e.g., \"premium\", \"standard\", \"basic\")\n- **Benefit**: Flexible, doesn't require new Elasticsearch features\n- **Extensibility**: Supports any tier classification scheme\n\n---\n\n## Integration Points with Elasticsearch v8.17.0\n\n### Core Classes Referenced\n1. `ShardsAllocator` - Interface implemented by PriorityShardsAllocator\n2. `BalancedShardsAllocator` - Wrapped/delegated by PriorityShardsAllocator\n3. `AllocationDecider` - Extended by PriorityAllocationDecider\n4. `AllocationDeciders` - Composition layer for decider chain\n5. `IndexMetadata` - Source of priority settings\n6. `RoutingAllocation` - Context for allocation decisions\n7. `ClusterSettings` - For dynamic configuration updates\n8. `RoutingNode` - Node representation in allocation\n\n### Settings Defined (Following Elasticsearch Patterns)\n- **Index-scoped**: `index.routing.allocation.priority`\n- **Cluster-scoped**: `cluster.routing.allocation.priority.*`\n- **Dynamic**: All settings support runtime updates\n- **Type-safe**: Using Setting<T> framework\n\n---\n\n## Next Steps for Implementation Team\n\n### Phase 1: Foundation (Estimated 1-2 weeks)\n- [ ] Add `INDEX_ROUTING_ALLOCATION_PRIORITY_SETTING` to IndexMetadata\n- [ ] Implement priority extraction from index settings\n- [ ] Implement node tier classification from node attributes\n- [ ] Register PriorityAllocationDecider in AllocationService initialization\n\n### Phase 2: Core Allocator (Estimated 2-3 weeks)\n- [ ] Implement shard queue reordering in PriorityShardsAllocator\n- [ ] Implement decision logic in PriorityAllocationDecider\n- [ ] Register Placement in the decider chain\n   - Interaction patterns with DiskThresholdDecider, AwarenessAllocationDecider\n   - Graceful degradation when premium capacity exhausted\n\n6. **API Changes & Configuration** (Section 5)\n   - New index setting for priority\n   - New cluster settings for feature control\n   - Cluster Explain API enhancements\n   - Configuration examples with YAML/JSON\n\n7. **Implementation Roadmap** (Section 6)\n   - 4-phase development plan (Foundation, Allocator, Integration, Polish)\n   - Clear milestones for each phase\n\n8. **Behavior Examples** (Section 7)\n   - 3 realistic scenarios with expected allocation outcomes\n   - Example 1: Simple priority allocation\n   - Example 2: Awareness + priority interaction\n   - Example 3: Degradation under load\n\n9. **Backward Compatibility** (Section 8)\n   - Default behavior unchanged\n   - Opt-in activation\n   - Upgrade path\n\n10. **Observability & Debugging** (Section 9)\n    - Logging strategy\n    - Explain API integration\n    - Future metrics\n\n11. **Security Considerations** (Section 10)\n    - Index priority as user input handling\n    - Resource starvation mitigation\n    - Audit trail\n\n12. **Performance Considerations** (Section 11)\n    - Shard queue sorting: O(n log n)\n    - Decider invocation: O(1) per shard-node pair\n    - Overall negligible impact\n\n13. **Testing Strategy** (Section 12)\n    - Unit tests, integration tests, chaos tests\n    - Multi-scenario testing plans\n\n14. **Success Criteria** (Section 14)\n    - Design review checklist (✅ all complete)\n    - Implementation phase next steps\n\n---\n\n## Deliverable #2: PriorityShardsAllocator Skeleton\n**File**: `PriorityShardsAllocator.java` (448 lines)\n\n### Key Features:\n1. **Implements ShardsAllocator Interface**\n   - ✅ `allocate(RoutingAllocation)` - sync allocation with priority sorting\n   - ✅ `allocate(RoutingAllocation, ActionListener<Void>)` - async variant\n   - ✅ `execute(RoutingAllocation, AllocationCommands, ...)` - command execution\n   - ✅ `decideShardAllocation(ShardRouting, RoutingAllocation)` - explain API support\n\n2. **Configuration Settings**\n   - `CLUSTER_ROUTING_ALLOCATION_PRIORITY_ENABLED` - feature toggle\n   - `CLUSTER_ROUTING_ALLOCATION_PRIORITY_PREMIUM_TIERS` - tier classification\n   - `CLUSTER_ROUTING_ALLOCATION_PRIORITY_THRESHOLD` - priority threshold\n\n3. **Core Methods**\n   - `sortUnassignedShardsByPriority()` - reorders shard queue by priority\n   - `getIndexPriority()` - extracts priority from IndexMetadata\n   - `isNodePremiumTier()` - classifies node as premium/standard\n   - Configuration update listeners for dynamic changes\n\n4. **Documentation**\n   - Setter methods for testing\n\n---\n\n## Deliverable #3: PriorityAllocationDecider Skeleton\n**File**: `PriorityAllocationDecider.java` (458 lines)\n\n### Key Features:\n1. **Extends AllocationDecider Base Class**\n   - ✅ `canAllocate(ShardRouting, RoutingNode, RoutingAllocation)` - main decision logic\n   - ✅ `canRemain(IndexMetadata, ShardRouting, RoutingNode, RoutingAllocation)` - residency decision\n   - Uses return Decision.YES, Decision.THROTTLE, or Decision.NO\n\n2. **Decision Logic**\n   ```\n   if feature disabled:\n       return YES (always allow)\n\n   if priority < threshold:\n       return YES (no constraint)\n\n   if priority >= threshold:\n       if node is premium:\n           return YES (preferred allocation)\n       else:\n           return THROTTLE (allow but prefer premium)\n   ```\n\n3. **Configuration Settings**\n   - `CLUSTER_ROUTING_ALLOCATION_PRIORITY_ENABLED` - feature toggle\n   - `CLUSTER_ROUTING_ALLOCATION_PRIORITY_PREMIUM_TIERS` - tier list\n   - `CLUSTER_ROUTING_ALLOCATION_PRIORITY_THRESHOLD` - soft constraint threshold\n\n4. **Core Methods**\n   - `getIndexPriority()` - extracts priority from IndexMetadata\n   - `isNodePremiumTier()` - checks node tier attribute\n   - Configuration update listeners for dynamic changes\n\n5. **Documentation**\n   - Comprehensive JavaDoc with decision logic explanation\n   - Detailed class documentation with usage examples\n   - Example scenarios with expected outcomes\n   - Integration notes with other deciders\n\n6. **Error Handling & Logging**\n   - Graceful degradation with sensible defaults\n   - Trace-level logging for each decision\n   - Debug logging for configuration updates\n\n---\n\n## Deliverable #4: Overview & Integration Guide\n**File**: `README.md` (314 lines)\n\n### Contents:\n1. **Project Overview** - Summary of the design\n2. **Deliverables Summary** - Description of all components\n3. **Architecture Diagram** - Visual representation of component integration\n4. **Design Highlights** - Key principles and decisions\n5. **Configuration Examples**\n   - Enabling priority allocation (cluster settings)\n   - Configuring node tiers (elasticsearch.yml)\n   - Setting index priorities (index settings API)\n6. **Usage Scenarios**\n   - Scenario 1: Critical application index\n   - Scenario 2: Multi-tier allocation strategy\n7. **References** - Links to relevant Elasticsearch source files\n8. **Implementation Roadmap** - 4-phase development plan with status\n9. **File Structure** - Layout of deliverables\n10. **Success Criteria** - Design phase completion checklist\n11. **Notes for Implementers** - Guidance for development team\n\n---\n\n## Design Quality Metrics\n\n| Aspect | Coverage |\n|--------|----------|\n| Architecture Documentation | ✅ Complete (10 sections) |\n| Existing Code References | ✅ 6 key Elasticsearch classes analyzed |\n| Integration Points | ✅ 3 existing deciders covered |\n| Configuration Options | ✅ 6 new settings defined |\n| Behavior Examples | ✅ 3 detailed scenarios provided |\n| Error Handling | ✅ Graceful degradation strategy |\n| Backward Compatibility | ✅ Opt-in, defaults unchanged |\n| Testing Strategy | ✅ Unit, integration, chaos plans |\n| Security Considerations | ✅ 3 aspects addressed |\n| Performance Analysis | ✅ Complexity analysis provided |\n| Code Documentation | ✅ Comprehensive JavaDoc |\n\n---\n\n## Key Design Decisions\n\n### 1. Two-Component Architecture\n- **PriorityShardsAllocator**: Reorders shards (macro-level)\n- **PriorityAllocationDecider**: Makes node-level decisions (micro-level)\n- **Benefit**: Clean separation of concerns, composable with existing components\n\n### 2. Soft Constraint via THROTTLE\n- High-priority shards on standard nodes return THROTTLE (not NO)\n- **Benefit**: Graceful degradation when premium capacity exhausted\n- **Safety**: No starvation of critical indices\n\n### 3. Dynamic Configuration\n- All settings cluster-scoped and dynamic\n- **Benefit**: No restart required; supports A/B testing\n- **Simplicity**: Follows Elasticsearch patterns\n\n### 4. Non-Intrusive Integration\n- Wraps BalancedShardsAllocator (decorator pattern)\n- Extends AllocationDecider (template method pattern)\n- **Benefit**: No modification of existing code\n- **Safety**: Minimal risk of regression\n\n### 5. Node Tier via Attributes\n- Uses existing `node.attr.*` mechanism\n- Operators define tier names (e.g., \"premium\", \"standard\", \"basic\")\n- **Benefit**: Flexible, doesn't require new Elasticsearch features\n- **Extensibility**: Supports any tier classification scheme\n\n---\n\n## Integration Points with Elasticsearch v8.17.0\n\n### Core Classes Referenced\n1. `ShardsAllocator` - `AllocationDeciders` - Composition layer for decider chain\n5. `IndexMetadata` - Source of priority settings\n6. `RoutingAllocation` - Context for allocation decisions\n7. `ClusterSettings` - For dynamic configuration updates\n8. `RoutingNode` - Node representation in allocation\n\n### Settings Defined (Following Elasticsearch Patterns)\n- **Index-scoped**: `index.routing.allocation.priority`\n- **Cluster-scoped**: `cluster.routing.allocation.priority.*`\n- **Dynamic**: All settings support runtime updates\n- **Type-safe**: Using Setting<T> framework\n\n---\n\n## Next Steps for Implementation Team\n\n### Phase 1: Foundation (Estimated 1-2 weeks)\n- [ ] Add `INDEX_ROUTING_ALLOCATION_PRIORITY_SETTING` to IndexMetadata\n- [ ] Implement High-priority shards still allocate to standard nodes if premium exhausted |\n| **Dynamic Configuration** | All settings cluster-scoped, no restart required |\n| **Backward Compatible** | Feature opt-in, all indices default to priority=50 |\n| **Observable** | Detailed logging (TRACE, DEBUG, INFO) + Explain API support |\n| **Safe** | Error handling with fallbacks to defaults |\n\n---\n\n## 📋 Design Architecture\n\n```\nAllocationService\n    ↓\nPriorityShardsAllocator (NEW)\n    ├─ Sort shards by priority (highest first)\n    └─ Delegate to BalancedShardsAllocator\n        ↓\n        AllocationDeciders Chain\n        ├─ EnableAllocationDecider\n        ├─ DiskThresholdDecider\n        ├─ AwarenessAllocationDecider\n        ├─ FilterAllocationDecider\n        ├─ PriorityAllocationDecider (NEW)\n        └─ ... other deciders ...\n```\n\n---\n\n## ✨ Design Highlights\n\n1. **Index-Level Priority**: Set per-index via `index.routing.allocation.priority` (0-100)\n2. **Node Tier Classification**: Tag nodes with `node.attr.allocation_tier` (e.g., \"premium\")\n3. **Soft Constraints**: Use THROTTLE decision for preference, not requirement\n4. **Cluster Settings**: `cluster.routing.allocation.priority.{enabled,premium_tiers,threshold}`\n5. **Integration**: Placed in decider chain after hard constraints, before other preferences\n\n---\n\n## 📚 Code
